# Task backlog — the refill ladder (COMPLETE work ledger)

Owner directive (Robin, 2026-07-06): **4–5 lanes working constantly.** Fires refill from this ladder per fire.md §2E — and when a ladder runs dry, an idle fire AUTHORS the next master itself from the spec slice + evidence chain (FIRE-AUTHORED header, one per fire). Only three things escalate to the attended session: a missing spec, a design fork, or a canon question (PIPELINE-DRY flag). Robin authors nothing and verdicts everything.
**Completeness law (owner ask, 2026-07-06 evening): every known work item lives HERE — queued, gated, owner-owed, or explicitly retired to Stale. If work is discovered anywhere (review finding, fire flag, playtest, spec slice), it gets a ladder line in the same commit.** The dashboard renders this file; an item missing here is invisible, and invisible = forgotten (Demo Day taught us).

**GOAL REGISTRATION LAW (owner ask, 2026-07-16): every authored master adds its leaf to `tasks/goals.json` in the same commit; every drain updates that leaf's status and merge hash.** A task is not fully authored or drained until both the backlog and goal tree tell the same truth.

**Stale-check law**: a master written >2 days ago gets a 60-second reality check against current main before queueing (the m4-02 "6→40 refresh" lesson) — refresh it or flag it, never blind-queue.

- ⚠️ **F-1033-2 (REAL, correction — TWO FIRES CARRIED A FALSE BLOCKER, AND LANE-D'S REFRESH IS SMALLER THAN THE BOARD BELIEVES).** s1031 diagnosed `tasks/stream-capture-duty.md` as unqueueable and s1032 "re-confirmed" it; both recorded, in the same words, that it *"references a non-existent `scripts/stream-sync.mjs`"*. **s1033 probed the file rather than the claim, and that third defect is FALSE.** The master never writes a `.mjs` extension — it says bare **"stream-sync"** twice, at `:11` (*"run stream-sync dry-check"*) and `:13` (*"~/GoldRushStream staging via stream-sync only"*) — and **`scripts/stream-sync.sh` EXISTS**: a substantive 105-line syncer that reads `assets/stream/loop-manifest.json`, stages to `$HOME/GoldRushStream/loop`, and guards on `jq` + a runnable `ffmpeg`. The reference is **valid**; the predecessors inferred an extension the master never wrote and then found it missing. **Classic Mistake #4 (The Stale Belief), and it propagated because the second fire "verified" by re-reading the first fire's sentence.** *A claim of the form "X does not exist" must be settled by `ls`/`find` on X's real name, never by pattern-matching a path out of prose.* **The two REAL defects stand, both re-verified first-hand:** (1) **no pre-flight line of any kind** — `grep -c "Pre-flight\|SAFE-DUPE"` = **0**, the F-1027-1 brick class, and the reason a blind re-queue would be unsafe; (2) scope-1's deliverable **`scripts/stream-capture.mjs` already exists on disk** (13,021 bytes, Jul 12), while scope-1 is still written in create-it voice — a **RECURRING** duty (its own header says *"re-queue on every era gate + weekly refresh"*) phrased as a one-time build, so a runner will either no-op or rewrite a working script. **THE REFRESH IS THEREFORE TWO EDITS, NOT THREE:** add a lane-d pre-flight (SAFE-DUPE template), and re-voice scope-1 from *build the capture script* to *run the existing `scripts/stream-capture.mjs` and refresh the manifest*, naming `scripts/stream-sync.sh` **with its extension** so this misreading cannot recur. **Not authored by s1033** — §2E's one-authored-master cap was spent on `lane-resource-timing-false-greens.md`. GATE: ~~lane-d refills once the master carries a pre-flight and a recurring-voice scope-1.~~ **SUPERSEDED by F-1034-1 (s1034) — the prescribed re-voice was itself the superseded instruction; lane-d refills on this ladder only after Robin rules on the gameplay feed.**
- ⛔ **F-1034-1 (REAL, BLOCKING — THE PRESCRIBED FIX WAS THE BUG, AND THE STREAM LOOP IS RUNNING ON 3.4 MINUTES OF CONTENT WITH ZERO GAMEPLAY IN IT).** s1034 went to apply F-1033-2's two edits and found that the second one — *"re-voice scope-1 from build the capture script to **run the existing `scripts/stream-capture.mjs` and refresh the manifest**"* — orders a runner to break **two owner rulings made two days after that master was written.** Verified at file:line, first-hand, none of it inherited: **(1) HEADLESS BAN** (`tasks/stream-rotation-curator.md:15`, owner 2026-07-14, verbatim: *"HEADLESS CAPTURES ARE BANNED from the pools (owner: 'gameplay is very slow' — headless browsers throttle the frame loop): only real-speed footage enters (owner OBS recordings, or future headed captures on a virtual display)"*; echoed `docs/marketing/BRAND-BOOK.md:26` *"Gameplay footage is REAL-SPEED only"*) — and scope 1 **is** a headless capture, `scripts/stream-capture.mjs:38,117` = `chromium.launch({ headless: true })`. **(2) PLAYLIST LAW / one-writer** (`:16`, same ruling: *"The curator only ever edits the manifest"*) — scope 4 orders this duty to write `loop-manifest.json` (`stream-capture.mjs:180-183`) while `scripts/stream-curate.mjs:194` **fully regenerates** it from its pools. That collision is not hypothetical: **it already fired** — the Jul-12 run's gameplay + slideshow entries were erased by the Jul-14 regenerate, which is why they are absent from the manifest today (`grep -c` = **0** for both; ceremony = **1**). **(3) The output is unreachable regardless of the laws:** the curator's only pool for `artifacts/stream-capture/` accepts `/^ceremony-[^.]+\.(mp4|webm)$/` (`stream-curate.mjs:43`), so `node scripts/stream-curate.mjs --check` prints `IGNORED artifacts/stream-capture/capture-duty-gameplay.mp4 (not an approved ceremony-recording)`. **Re-running scopes 1-2 today is a guaranteed no-op with a green report — Mistake #1 with a coat on.** **THE MEASURED PRODUCT STATE (curator `--check`, read-only, s1034):** the live program is **15 items / 3.41 min total**, mix **finished-game-footage 0.00 min = 0.0%**, era-art-card 51.4%, era-art-reel 44.2%, ceremony 4.4%, with the curator itself logging **`SHORT program: 2195.7s below target; approved unique pool exhausted`**. The sole gameplay pool (`marketing/raw/stream`, `.webm`) holds **only the four 2026-07-10 webms, all banned by `stream-curate.mjs:86`** — so a 24/7 channel is looping ~3.4 minutes of cards and reels ~420×/day and **has never shown a second of the game.** **THE DUTY CHAIN IS ALSO DORMANT:** `docs/marketing/STREAMING.md:16` assigns *"Run `node scripts/stream-curate.mjs`, `scripts/stream-sync.sh`, then `scripts/stream-watchdog.sh` each factory fire cycle"* — but **that duty was never written into `scripts/fire.md`**, so no fire has ever run it: **`logs/stream-health.log` does not exist** (`find logs -name "stream*"` = empty) and `~/GoldRushStream/loop/` was last staged **2026-07-14 11:40, eleven days ago**, while the manifest itself moved Jul-15 — *the staged loop is stale against its own manifest.* **ACTIONS TAKEN:** `tasks/stream-capture-duty.md` carries a ⛔ DO-NOT-QUEUE banner naming which scopes died (1, 4) and which survive (3 ceremony; 5's honesty gate), so no fire or attended session re-queues the trap. **NOT taken, deliberately:** I did **not** run curate/sync — regenerating the manifest and restaging the owner's live loop is a write to what the family watches, the chain is not in my law file, and the 0%-gameplay hole it would not fix is the owner's call anyway. **lane-d stays PIPELINE-DRY; §2E's HARD LIMIT forbids authoring here** (the replacement gameplay path — headed capture on a virtual display vs. owner OBS recordings — is a design fork with an infra choice in it).

🗄️ **SHOULD A SAVED PROFILE THAT PREDATES PROFILE-SCOPED STORAGE BE RESCUED, OR LEFT BEHIND? (F-1113-5, raised s1113, put on this desk s1114 — triage, not a fire.)** ✓ VERIFIED by a runner's own storage probe, quoted verbatim: *"after reload the raw epoch existed but the scoped key did not; migration did not create it."* **Nothing is broken today and nothing is waiting on you to unblock a merge** — the four fixtures that surfaced this were fixed at `b43b31ad` by seeding the key the product actually reads, so they are correct under every hypothesis. **The open question is product-side and small but real:** `migrateProfileDataKeys()` (`ProfileStorage.ts:342-350`) does **not** move an existing *raw* profile-data key to its profile-scoped name. So a player whose browser holds a pre-scoping key keeps it, while the game reads the scoped one and sees a fresh profile. **Whether that is right depends on a call only you can make:** say **"rescue"** and a fire teaches migration to adopt a raw key when no scoped key exists *(this is the player-friendly reading — an old save survives the upgrade)*; say **"leave it"** and we record that pre-scoping keys are deliberately abandoned, and no future fixture or feature is allowed to lean on migration to save them *(also defensible — it keeps one key format and avoids resurrecting stale state)*. ⛔ **A fire did not just pick one because it changes what happens to real saved player data on upgrade** (§7.3). **Cost either way: one small slice, same day.** Note the honest limit on the evidence: this was observed in a test fixture's storage, not reproduced against a real aged player profile — worth one probe before implementing whichever you choose.

The only casualty is the `061-first-claim-onboarding` suite, red for seven days (3 of 4 tests, both projects) because it hard-codes `'Marta Vale'`. **THE CALL: (a) "Rowan"** — keep today's accidental-but-coherent behaviour and relax the test to accept either whitelisted greeter *(recommended: it matches the engine's own long-standing two-candidate contract, and the elder pointing you toward the tavern is the more natural line — but it does ratify a side effect nobody designed)*; **or (b) "Marta"** — restore the greeter the feature shipped with, by pinning `firstClaimGreeter()` to the tavernkeeper *(the trail already leads to her, so the welcome and the destination would be one person)*. **Either is a one-line change plus a test edit, same day.** A fire did not just pick one because it decides whose voice welcomes every new player on the E1 release path — §7.3, yours. - ⚠️ **F-1027-1 (REAL, root-caused, ONE-OFF — a corrective is owed to the SKILL, not to the code) — A PARAPHRASED PRE-FLIGHT BRICKED A LANE.** `tasks/lane-m1-m2-resource-guards.md` (s1026, fire-authored) carried a pre-flight headed "SAFE-DUPE" whose body said the OPPOSITE of the template: *"Confirm the lane branch holds nothing unmerged: `git log main..HEAD --oneline` must be EMPTY. If it is NOT empty, STOP and report."* On this board that is a **permanent brick** — lanes land by SQUASH merge, so a lane branch reads ahead FOREVER once its work merges, and `main..HEAD` is essentially never empty. Codex obeyed it exactly, correctly refused to reset over what looked like undrained work, and stopped (log `logs/runs-archive/20260725-105857-lane-a-lane-m1-m2-resource-guards.md.log`, 35,105 tokens, zero files changed). **The canonical template in `.claude/skills/author-task/SKILL.md` §3 is CORRECT and always was** — it says "the lane branch being ahead is NORMAL", classifies an already-merged ahead commit as a SAFE DUPE, and prescribes `git checkout -B <lane-branch> main && git clean -fd`. The failure was purely that a fire did not copy it verbatim, despite §3's own instruction ("copy VERBATIM") and §6's checklist item ("pre-flight verbatim for the slot ✓"). **BOUNDED, measured — this is NOT an epidemic:** the defective wording appears in exactly **1** master (plus its own done-move copy); **~190** other lane masters carry the correct "SAFE DUPE" wording. GATE (owed, attended — a fire is permission-denied on `.claude/`, see F-1027-4): paste the incident warning into SKILL.md §3 under the LANE template so the next author sees what the paraphrase cost. **Standing lesson, already law but newly paid for: "ahead" is a lane's normal resting state; the only real question is "is this tip's content on main?", answered by file-probe/diff, never by an ahead-count (§5.16).**

- 🔴 **F-1027-2 (REAL, MATERIAL, owner-relevant — THE RETENTION LAW'S MIRROR IS A BLACK HOLE).** `.gitignore:7` is a bare **`*.log`**, and every file in `logs/runs-archive/` is named `*.md.log` — so the directory the RETENTION LAW (§4.10b, owner's words, written 2026-07-25) names as *the tracked mirror* tracks **nothing**. Verified four ways, not inferred: `git ls-files logs/runs-archive` → **0**; `ls` → **48 files, 205MB**; `git log --all -- "logs/runs-archive/*"` → **EMPTY (no commit in the entire repo history has ever tracked one)**; and the law's own commit `e4f23634` touched only 3 files (CLAUDE.md, dashboard-gen.sh, BACKLOG.md), adding **zero** logs. The BACKLOG line above claims "tracked mirror seeded (45)" — that claim is false and has been corrected in place. `scripts/dashboard-gen.sh:71-76` faithfully copies each new `tasks/runs/*.log` into the mirror every regen, and every copy lands **ignored**. **Exposure (stated precisely, not dramatised): nothing is lost yet — all 48 logs are on disk, and the run METADATA is safe, because `logs/task-stats.jsonl` IS tracked and does record every run (stamp/lane/task/tokens/minutes). What is unprotected is the log TEXT — the reasoning, the verdicts, the evidence of what each Codex run actually did. It dies with the disk (Mistake #11), which is exactly what the retention + backup laws exist to prevent.** **I did NOT fix it unilaterally**, because the obvious fix (`!logs/runs-archive/` + `git add`) commits **205MB of raw Codex transcripts** permanently into history on Robin's slow line — irreversible without a force-push, which is deny-listed (§7.7), after two fires already hit multi-minute push timeouts. **RECOMMENDATION, measured: track a COMPACTED mirror, which §4.10b explicitly blesses ("compaction of TRACKED files is lawful").** A 300-line tail of the largest log (26MB) is **21.5KB**, and the tail is precisely where the verdict/report lives (it is how s1027 proved today's no-op honest) → a tail-mirror of all 48 is **~1MB instead of 205MB**, preserving every conclusion. Gzip is the alternative (~10-20MB, keeps every byte). **OWNER'S DESK: one word on which — tails (~1MB, recommended) or gzip (~15MB, complete).** 📈 **MEASUREMENT REFRESHED s1056 (the question is unchanged; the exposure is not):** `logs/runs-archive/` is now **71 files / 238 MB** (was 48 / 205 MB at s1027) and **`git ls-files` returns exactly 1** — `CODEX-WALL-20260725-0923Z-…md`, which is tracked only because it is the one file there that is not named `*.log`. So the `.gitignore:7` bare `*.log` still swallows the entire mirror, and it has swallowed **~33 MB more** while the format question waits. Nothing is lost yet (all 71 are on disk; `logs/task-stats.jsonl` still carries the metadata). Recommendation unchanged and now cheaper in relative terms: **tails**. ✅ **Verified same fire that the RETENTION LAW's other half is holding**: all three prunes §4.10b named as owed removal are **gone** — `lane-runner-v3.sh:117-118`, `lane-runner-v2.sh:77-78` and `fire-runner.sh:86-89` each retain the deleted `find … -mtime … -delete` line as a commented epitaph. Nothing is deleting run logs today.

- **F-1027-3 (real, LOW severity, no data loss) — the durable ledger is ~99% duplicate rows.** `logs/task-stats.jsonl` holds **5,931 lines but only 39 unique stamps**; the worst single stamp is repeated **508×**, today's no-op **35×**. The dashboard's dedupe (`seen` set keyed on `stamp`, `dashboard-gen.sh:78+`) is not holding, so rows re-append on regens. Harmless to correctness — every fact is present, just repeated — and this is a TRACKED file, so compaction is lawful and git keeps every prior version. Owed (small, any idle fire/attended): fix the dedupe key, then one compaction pass. Flagged because this is the ledger F-1027-2 shows we are actually relying on. ➡️ ✅ **CLOSED — ALREADY FIXED IN `s1028`, AND THIS LINE HAS BEEN ADVERTISING OWED WORK EVER SINCE (s1056 verified both ways).** Read the code *and* the data rather than either alone: `dashboard-gen.sh:90-129` carries the s1028 fix with the three defects named in its own comment (json imported before the dedupe read; `except` narrowed to `(ValueError, KeyError, TypeError)` so a future programming error surfaces instead of silently disabling dedupe; key is **`stamp|lane|task`**, not `stamp`; and the ledger is **UPSERTED** best-row-per-key so a live run's `tokens=0` rows cannot freeze a run at zero). The file agrees: **69 lines, 59 unique stamps, 69 unique `stamp|lane|task` keys, 0 unparsable** — down from 5,931/39. **The 10 "duplicate" stamps are NOT duplicates**: they are sibling lanes starting in the same second (e.g. `20260723-060506` = art + lane-a + lane-c + lane-d, four different tasks), which is exactly why the s1028 fix widened the key — a stamp-only key would have *permanently dropped* them. No compaction owed; nothing to fix. **This is the Mistake #5/#8 hazard in ledger form** (same shape s1053 flagged on F-1049-2): a closed finding left reading OPEN is an invitation for a future fire to re-author against working code.

- **F-1027-4 (environmental, for the owner's allowlist decision — extends F-1024-4).** A headless fire is **permission-denied on writes to `.claude/`**, so a fire cannot repair its own playbooks/skills even when it has just diagnosed the exact defect (F-1027-1's one-line SKILL.md warning is owed to an attended session purely for this reason). Also re-confirmed denied this fire: `git check-ignore`, and `git cherry` — the latter is the precise tool for "is this branch's patch already in main", i.e. exactly the false-ahead question the board asks every single fire; without it the answer takes 4 commands (file-list compare + content diff + file-probe). Worth adding both to the fire allowlist alongside `npm install`.

- ✅ **F-1046-1 (opened AND FIXED s1046) — the Retention Law's tracked mirror was archiving fossils, and the trap has now caught two fires.** ✓ VERIFIED at `scripts/dashboard-gen.sh:73-76`: the mirror loop was `if not os.path.exists(_d): shutil.copy2(_p,_d)`. That script regenerates **every ~60 s**, so the first copy fires **seconds into a live run** and captures only Codex's **14-line header** — and the existence guard then **freezes that fossil permanently, forever.** The consequence is the exact inverse of the law's intent: the **tracked, git-durable** copy (the one §4.10b exists to guarantee) was a stub, while the only complete report sat in **untracked `tasks/runs/`**. Measured on ED-04: archive **14 lines** vs a **2,490,071-byte** live log. **Not new and not lane-specific — F-1038 hit it at 410 KB vs 644 KB and explicitly wrote it up as *"A TRAP FOR THE NEXT READER"*, and it then caught s1046**, which read the fossil, concluded "lane-c run reports are being lost", drafted that as a finding with a corrective task attached, and only caught it by re-reading F-1038 before committing. **A documented trap is not a fixed trap; the write-up cost two fires what a one-line guard would have cost nobody.** FIX: re-copy whenever the source is newer or a different size, so a run's archive converges on the complete log as it finishes. **Verified after the fix by measurement — the ED-04 archive copy is now 2,490,071 bytes, byte-matching the live log.** No data was ever destroyed (the `+3d` prune that would have made this fatal was already removed by s1033/F-1028-3 under the same law) — the exposure was that **git's history was a stub**, which is Mistake #11 wearing the Retention Law's coat. **Standing lesson: `logs/runs-archive/` was a snapshot, never a mirror — any fire diagnosing a run must read `tasks/runs/` (live, complete) and treat a suspiciously short archive as the archiver's defect, not the runner's.**

Identical shape to F-1054-1 (headline narrower than the question above it), which is the argument for fixing the *class*: s1054 fixed *which files get asked*; this fixes *what the asking means*. **FIX: every in-git blob is now also tested against the objects reachable from the remote-tracking refs** (one `git rev-list --objects --remotes` traversal — **~150 ms, 42,475 objects** — not a query per file), and anything held only here prints as **LOCAL-ONLY (in git HERE, but on no origin ref — one push from safe)**. **Kept deliberately SEPARATE from AT RISK:** at-risk bytes are unrecoverable, local-only bytes are one `git push` from safe, and merging the buckets would hide *which of the two acts is owed*. `--strict` now exits 1 on either; **default stays exit 0**, so a routine audit still can never block a drain. **END-TO-END PROOF, not an assertion edit — the same fire watched the number move by the act the tool prescribes:** before the push `LOCAL-ONLY 14 files / 36.54 MB, --strict exit 1`; the push landed (`origin/save/art-diverged-20260726` = local tip `97955b59`, verified by `git rev-parse` on both); after, `LOCAL-ONLY 0 files / 0 bytes, --strict exit 0`. **Nothing was reclassified sideways:** AT RISK read 0 before and after, and the SHIPPED/SALVAGED/DIVERGED counts (168 / 10 / 14) are untouched — the new bucket is orthogonal to the old five, exactly as intended. Gates: tsc clean · build green **1.51s** · both exit paths verified by spawn (1 → 0) · `--json` gains `localOnlyFiles`/`localOnlyBytes`/`localOnly`. **CAVEAT written into the file, because a green here is only as fresh as the last fetch:** remote-tracking refs are a local mirror; run `git fetch` first if another writer may have pushed the blobs in question. **This measurement is also what surfaced F-1055-2 below — the same blindness holds for every branch on this disk, not just the art staging folder.**

The arithmetic closes against this fire's own push: **798 blobs / 616.8 MB before it, 784 / 580.3 MB after — exactly the 14 files / 36.54 MB that went offsite**, which is the cheapest available proof the measurement is counting the right thing. That is **2.2× the hole F-1045-1 found**, reported as clean, by the tool F-1045-1 wrote to find exactly this — and s1053 had just written *"run this audit and report its count"* into `scripts/fire.md` §2E as **law**, so every future fire was about to read a false zero as compliance. **ROOT CAUSE: identity was decided by NAME and then by SIZE.** Neither answers "are these bytes recoverable?" — only the blob hash does. **FIX (`4051bf31`): hash is now the sole classifier**, with a new **EXPOSED** bucket (name on main, bytes are not) and an **AT RISK** total spanning `UNTRACKED + EXPOSED`; `--strict` exits 1 when anything is unbacked, default stays exit 0 so a routine audit can never block a drain. The old `SAME-SIZE` bucket's *"assumed shipped"* is gone too — **SHIPPED now means the hashes match, and it reclassified nothing (168 before, 168 after)**, which is the evidence that the change only revealed the unchecked bucket rather than moving the goalposts. Gates: tsc clean · build green 1.17s · both exit paths verified (0 / 1) · before/after run pasted in the handoff. **EXPOSURE CLOSED: all 14 preserved on `save/art-diverged-20260726` = `97955b59`**, built through a throwaway `GIT_INDEX_FILE` on the s1045 pattern — quarantined under `salvage/art-diverged-20260726/` so they can never be confused with shipped art; `git diff --shortstat main save/…` = **14 files changed, 132 insertions, 0 deletions**, main's tip and working tree untouched. **The audit now reports AT RISK 0 — a true zero, verified by re-running it after the salvage.** ⚖️ **THIS ADOPTS NOTHING: F-1044-3's adjudication is untouched and its recommended default (REJECT) still stands.** Preserving prejudges nothing; losing prejudges everything, and loss was the only irreversible option on the table. **Reverse with one word ("drop the diverged salvage branch") — veto window open (§7.4).** 🔎 **NEW ADJUDICATION EVIDENCE FOR F-1044-3, since I had the bytes in hand:** the Jul-25 run report `codex-art-run-art-e9-buildings-a.md` publishes a SHA-256 per file, and **all 5 staging `bld-*.png` match its PASS table exactly**, while **main's 5 blobs match none of them** — so the staging set is provably the artifact that run QA'd (not a partial or corrupted write), and the two sets are genuinely different images. The favicons are declared at `index.html:7-9` as `<link rel="icon">`, so the browser requests them during document parse, necessarily far before `firstFrameMs`. **Whenever a resource-timing entry for the favicon exists at all, `:223` fails with CERTAINTY.** It is not a timing draw, CPU load cannot move it, and **no number of isolated re-runs could ever have closed it** — the prescribed "cheap close" would have produced either a green that proved nothing or a red that was re-blamed on load. (The F-perf05-1 analogy that produced the misreading is a **real** load flake — but on `perf-01`, a different spec.) **OPEN, and handed to the runner as a measurement rather than guessed:** the favicon files date from **Jul 5**, *before* perf-05 shipped 2/2 green (s216 `8bd9eca`), so something drifted since — either headless Chromium's favicon-fetch behaviour changed under a browser/Playwright bump (the entry now exists where it used to be absent) or a `<link>`/filename moved. **DEFECT 2, and this one may be REAL:** `prefetchedBeforeWaveSpawn` is **empty** on both projects, so `:224` (`.length > 0`) would fail too — it is simply never reached. That assertion guards **perf-05's own shipped deliverable**, the wave-1 prefetch of `bld-sentry-beacon/palisade/sluice-works/stockpile-yard/signal-turret`, and the measured window is **wide** (desktop 2592→6133ms, ~3.5s) with nothing in it. It post-dates both measured runs (those artifacts show all 5 prefetch rows, which the route makes impossible), so it was an interrupted *still-bites* control for `:224` — **removed**, because shipping it would have made `:224` permanently and invisibly false. **F-1038-4 — DEFECT 2 RESOLVED, AND IT IS THE 250-ENTRY CAP AGAIN, NOT A PRODUCT REGRESSION.** With the buffer raised, all five buildings appear on **every** run (script-initiated ~2996ms, img-initiated ~3062ms). - 🔬 **F-1039-1 (OPEN, pre-existing, NOT caused by any recent slice — the residue the s1039 A/B exposed).** `perf-05-startup:231` (`expect(ttiMs).toBeLessThan(3_000)`) **fails on unmodified `main`** at load ≳4 and passes at load ≲3 — measured, not inferred: main scored **2874 / 3876 / 4392 / 3566 / 3643** across five interleaved desktop runs while the lane variant scored **3224 / 2947 / 3386 / 3510 / 3771**. The assertion is a **bare wall-clock threshold with no load precondition**, on a box that also runs Codex lanes, so its verdict is a function of what else the factory happens to be doing. That is why it drifted through four fires wearing three different explanations (F-1034-3 "threshold-shaped", F-1037-1 "refuted, the artifacts pass", F-1038-1 "load-sensitive, hold"). **DO NOT WIDEN THE THRESHOLD — F-1026-1 class**; a bigger number would buy silence, not truth, and the 3000ms bar is a real product commitment from `8bd9eca`. The two honest repairs, both needing an attended/owner call on which: **(a)** give the spec a **run precondition** — `--workers=1` plus a skip-with-a-loud-reason when `pgrep -f "codex exec"` is non-empty, so the gate refuses to render a verdict it cannot support; or **(b)** assert on a **load-normalised or best-of-N statistic** rather than a single draw. **(a) is the recommendation** — it keeps the product bar intact and makes the gate honest about when it can speak. **NOT fire-authorable blind** (it changes what "green" means for a shipped perf commitment = design-adjacent).
- 🧭 **F-1039-2 (process, OPEN — cheap, and it protects the whole factory).** Handoff stamps are being **hand-computed and drifting into the future**: s1038's line-1 reads `2026-07-25T11:00Z` but its handoff commit is `17:14:05+07:00` = **10:14Z**, a stamp **46 minutes ahead of real time**; the same fire reported lane-c as running "~70 min" when its run file was 15 minutes old. Harmless this once, because s1038 **cleared** its lock — but §1 tells every fire to **EXIT SILENTLY** while an `ACTIVE` lock is <45 min old, so **a future-dated `ACTIVE` stamp left by a fire that then dies stalls the entire factory for the skew plus 45 minutes**, and the staleness law's own clock is the thing that would be lying. **FIX (one line, any fire):** stamps come from `date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%MZ`, never from arithmetic — s1039's lock did exactly this and lands within 10s of true UTC. Worth a line in `scripts/fire.md` §1 so it binds rather than depends on each fire noticing.
- 📋 **F-1032-1 (NON-BLOCKING, opened s1032 by the drain above).** *The 250-entry Resource Timing buffer is a board-wide trap, not a Deepwater one.* Any spec that proves asset loading via `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')` degrades **silently** as the asset count grows — it does not fail loudly, it simply stops watching, and it reads green whenever its module happens to sort early. That is the F-1029-3 failure mode exactly; nothing makes Deepwater special except that it got caught. **GATE SATISFIED — s1032 ran the evidence pass in the same fire, and it is WORSE than the finding that prompted it. 12 call sites across 10 specs, and FOUR of them assert a NEGATIVE, where eviction produces a silent FALSE GREEN rather than a loud red.** F-1029-3 at least failed noisily. These four prove *"this did NOT load"* — laziness, dormancy and autoplay-gate guards — and an evicted entry makes `.some(...)` return false / the filtered array empty, so **the assertion passes for exactly the reason it was written to catch**:
  - `e2e/mu-02-music.spec.ts:49` — `era-e1-frontier-loop` must NOT be fetched before the gesture (**lazy-audio guard**).
  - `e2e/mu-03-era-audio.spec.ts:18` — `/era-e[123]-.*-loop/` must NOT be fetched on boot.
  - `e2e/mu-03-era-audio.spec.ts:35` — `title-theme` must NOT be fetched before the autoplay gate (**a browser-autoplay-policy guard — the kind of thing that costs a real player their first impression if it silently regresses**).
  - `e2e/e5-water-spike.spec.ts:89` — *"plain debug boot leaves the deepwater chunk dormant"* asserts `resources: []` for `/E5Deepwater/i`. **This one is in the SAME asset neighbourhood where s1032 MEASURED eviction happening today** (mobile's retained window ended around `DeepwaterArsenal.ts`), so for this site the risk is not hypothetical.
  The remaining 8 (`ed-01:141`, `ed-02:195`, `ed-03:269`, `ed-04:221`, `perf-05-startup:133`, `e7-playbook-spike:117`, `mu-02:24`, plus the now-fixed `e5-deepwater-claim:60`) are positive/poll-shaped and fail loudly, so they are the lesser half.
  **HONEST LIMIT ON THIS CLAIM:** the eviction *mechanism* is proven on this board (F-1029-3, measured), but **per-site exposure is UNVERIFIED** — each of the four runs at a different boot depth, and whether a plain boot reaches the 250-entry ceiling has not been measured. Do not report these as broken; report them as unproven. **The decisive one-line test, cheap and per-site: log the UNFILTERED `performance.getEntriesByType('resource').length` at the assertion point — if it is pinned at exactly 250, that guard is already vacuous.** Deliberately NOT authored as a master by s1032 (§2E one-authored-master-per-fire, spent on `lane-m2-05-readiness-seam.md`) — this is a ready, evidence-complete rung for the next fire, and the safest fix is the same one-liner that closed F-1029-3. **→ AUTHORED + QUEUED to lane-a by s1033 as `tasks/lane-resource-timing-false-greens.md` (goal leaf `resource-timing-false-greens`, registered in the same commit).** s1033 **re-verified every assertion DIRECTION at file:line before authoring** rather than carrying s1032's count forward, and all four hold: `mu-02-music:49` and `mu-03-era-audio:18`/`:35` are `.toBe(false)`; `e5-water-spike:87-90` is `.toEqual({ installed: false, resources: [] })`. **One nuance s1032 did not have, and it lowers the alarm on the loudest-sounding site:** `e5-water-spike:88` *also* asserts `installed: window.__GR_E5_DEEPWATER__ !== undefined` — a global, **not** eviction-dependent — so that half of the assertion still bites and site 4 is in fact the **least** exposed of the four, not the most. The master is built so the **measurement is the deliverable** (per-site unfiltered counts, both projects, before and after) rather than the patch, and it makes the high-value branch explicit and unmissable: **if raising the buffer makes an assertion FAIL, that is a REAL PRODUCT FINDING** — boot-critical audio, the autoplay gate, or a non-dormant deepwater chunk — and it must be reported, never reverted or loosened. The three init seams differ materially (`mu-02:7` block body; `mu-03:4` concise arrow needing conversion; `e5-water-spike` has **no init script at all** and navigates at `:85`), so each is named in the master; and the fix is required to be **pre-navigation**, since a buffer raised after `page.goto` recovers nothing — the one way this task could silently accomplish nothing. Firewalled OFF `m2-05-base-damage-repair.spec.ts` (lane-c LIVE in it) and off the reference spec. **The other 8 sites assert POSITIVES, where eviction fails loudly and is self-announcing — explicitly excluded as a separate, lower rung.**

- ✅ **F-1028-2 CLOSED — SHIPPED s1031 `63478dbb`: THE DASHBOARD'S "ALL-TIME" FIGURE NOW READS THE DURABLE LEDGER, AND THE EROSION IT WARNED ABOUT HAD ALREADY STARTED.** **Recorded here for the first time — a Completeness Law gap worth naming: F-1028-2 was raised by s1028 and carried by s1029 and s1030 as a "NEXT FIRE (D)" item, but it lived ONLY in STATUS handoff prose and was never written into this ledger.** Per CLAUDE.md §1, work that is not here does not exist; three fires deferred an item the board could not see. Finding: `scripts/dashboard-gen.sh` computed the owner-facing ALL-TIME headline by iterating **`tasks/runs/*.log`** — the very files `lane-runner-v3.sh:117` deletes with `-mtime +3 -delete`. So the number labelled ALL-TIME really meant *"the last ~3 days of logs that happened to survive pruning"*, and it was set to SHRINK. **THE FIX WAS VERIFIED BY A SAME-INSTANT A/B, NOT BY A BEFORE/AFTER IN TIME** (both sources move as runs tick, so a naive before/after would have proved nothing): old glob logic = **52 runs · 4,408 min · 23,832,282 tokens**; the ledger = **53 runs · 4,436 min · 24,719,013 tokens**. **The delta is one named, already-lost casualty:** `20260721-110240-lane-a-lane-town-variants-e8` (**886,731 tokens, 29 min**) is present in `logs/task-stats.jsonl` and **absent from `tasks/runs/`** — pruned on the 3-day window, and therefore already missing from the owner's headline before this fire. It now reappears in THE HUNGRIEST table, which is the visible proof the ledger source restores pruned history. Same fields, same arithmetic — **only the SOURCE moved**; the label now names it, so nothing is quietly re-based. A missing/empty ledger renders a LOUD `ALL-TIME: UNAVAILABLE … a missing source, not a zero` rather than a small number (F-1028-1's lesson: a silently degraded figure is indistinguishable from a real one). Tested by a full rc=0 run redirected into scratch outputs via `GOLD_RUSH_DASHBOARD_OUT`/`_GOAL_TREE_TMP`/`_HEALTH_LOG` so a bad build could never clobber the live board; goal tree rendered byte-identically (82,164 B), no placeholder. Scratch paths added to `.gitignore` as **exact paths, never wildcards**. **This does NOT close F-1028-3** — the §4.10b prune at `lane-runner-v3.sh:117` is still live and still owed removal; this fix only stops the prune from eating the owner's numbers.
- ✅ **F-1028-3 CLOSED s1033 — ALL THREE RETENTION-WINDOW PRUNES ARE GONE, AND THE FIX HAD TO BE DONE ON RUNNING PROCESSES WITHOUT TOUCHING THEM.** CLAUDE.md §4.10b named exactly three owed removals; **all three are now removed, verified by sweep** (`grep -rn "mtime +[0-9]* -delete" scripts/` returns only the commented records): **(1)** `lane-runner-v3.sh:117` — `find "$ROOT/tasks/runs" -name '*.log' -mtime +3 -delete`, the live one, deleting run history every 15 seconds; **(2)** `lane-runner-v2.sh:77` — the identical line, dormant but a loaded gun the moment anyone starts v2 (removed so a restart cannot silently resurrect the defect); **(3)** `fire-runner.sh:87` — `find logs -name 'fire-*.log' -mtime +14 -delete`, the fire logs, which are the factory's own memory of what each fire decided. Each was replaced by a **comment recording the exact deleted line and why it must not return**, so the next reader cannot "tidy" it back. **Line 116/76's `.git/*.stale*` + `tmp_obj_*` sweep was deliberately LEFT ALONE** — that is git's own scratch, not factory history, and §4.10b does not reach it. **THE METHOD IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING, because five fires correctly refused this edit on safety grounds and the safety concern was real:** bash reads a script incrementally by **byte offset**, so editing a running script in place shifts the offsets under the interpreter and can make it execute garbage — which is why §4.10b says *"edit only when the owning process is idle"*. But the runner was live (I had just queued lane-c attempt 2 to it) and `fire-runner.sh` is the script that **was executing this very fire**, so "wait for idle" had no window at all for two of the three. **Resolved with an atomic-rename swap instead of an in-place edit:** copy to an in-repo temp (same filesystem, so `mv` is a true `rename(2)`), edit the temp, `mv` it over the original — the running `bash` keeps its file descriptor on the **old inode** and finishes the version it started with, while the new version takes effect at the next fresh invocation. **Zero risk, no waiting, and it honours the law's intent (never corrupt a running runner) rather than only its letter.** Verified after each swap: both runner pids still alive, `tasks/running/` still holding the live lane-c task, `fire-runner.sh` tail intact through `exit 0`, file modes preserved (v3 `-rw-r--r--`, fire-runner `-rwxr-xr-x` — the exec bit survived). Diffs are comment-only replacements of a single command line, which bash never tokenizes, so the unavailability of `bash -n` (permission-gated for fires) cost no assurance. **Consequence to state plainly: `tasks/runs/` and `logs/fire-*.log` now grow without bound.** That is the intended trade — the owner ruled *"our history is our strength"*, and s1031 measured a real casualty (a 886,731-token run log already silently gone). The disk answer is **F-1027-2, still one word on the owner's desk ("tails" or "gzip")**, and it is now the only thing standing between the factory and unbounded log growth — **it just became the more urgent of his open questions.**

Raising 3→6 does not help if all six repeats live inside one invocation.**
- 🔬 **F-1036-1 (REAL, open — THE RESIDUE REPRODUCES; A LAWFUL NO-OP IS NOT A CLOSE).** `lane-m2-05-geometry-settle` attempt 1 took the master's own **pre-authorised** "0/6 twice in a row" exit and reported *"authorized measurement-only stop — 6/6, then 6/6 again"*. **That is NOT Mistake #1**: the master itself wrote that exit and the runner named it, and s1036 **verified the numbers rather than taking them** (codex rollout `019f987b`: two real `--repeat-each=6 --trace=off` invocations, `6 passed` in 58.4s/53.5s, real per-test durations, rc=0 in 197s; branch reset to main with `e2e/` + `src/` byte-identical by `diff -rq`, so zero diff and nothing at risk). **But s1036 then reproduced the defect on the same tree and machine: 2 fail / 6 at `:361`, correct F-1030-1 fingerprint.** Four batches, repo root: **A** (1st of session, default trace, warm vite) **2 FAIL/6** · B (`--trace=off`) 0/6 · C (default trace) 0/6 · D (`--trace=off`, vite dep cache parked aside) 0/6. **Two hypotheses killed so nobody re-derives them:** (1) *`--trace=off` is the lever* — **rejected**, A and C share the default flag and differ only in order; s1036 also self-corrects its own first read that *"tracing doubles runtime"* (A's 1.8m was failure-artifact writing — C ran the same config green in 58.8s). (2) *the vite dep-optimize cache is the cold thing* — **rejected** by batch D. **WHAT FITS EVERY SAMPLE:** `playwright.config.ts:20-25` starts a fresh dev server per **invocation**, but every repeat *inside* one invocation shares one browser launch and one warmed GLB cache. Line the invocation counts up and nobody was wrong or unlucky: s1035 = **3** invocations spread across a drain → **3** failures/12; attempt 1 = **2** back-to-back invocations straight after `npm install` + `build` → 0/12; s1036 batch A = **1 cold** invocation → **2** failures/6; s1036 B/C/D = 3 warm invocations → 0/18. **≈ one failure per COLD invocation; every warm invocation by anyone went green.** GATE: closed by the refreshed attempt 2's **scope-4 mutation control**, not by a green table. - 📐 **F-1036-2 (process, no task owed — AMENDS F-1035-2: THE BAR'S SHAPE, NOT ITS SIZE).** F-1035-2 raised the acceptance bar from `--repeat-each=3` to `≥6`. s1036's measurements say **the count was never the defect**: repeats inside a single `npx playwright test` invocation reuse one dev server, one browser launch and one warm cache, so `--repeat-each=N` buys **N samples of the fast variable and one draw of the slow one**. A 6-repeat batch can therefore read clean for exactly the reason a 3-repeat batch did. **Two rules for every master on the F-1030-3 timing list:** (1) the bar is **N separate invocations** (ideally not back-to-back), never one invocation with `--repeat-each=N`; (2) **stop trying to prove absence by repetition** — at ~1 failure per cold invocation, observation cannot distinguish *"fixed"* from *"the machine was warm"*, so the **deterministic can-it-still-fail control** (mutate → assertion goes red → revert → verify twice) is the acceptance evidence and green runs are corroboration only. That is the same principle that closed F-1026-1, F-1026-5, F-1029-3 and F-1032-1. Written into `lane-m2-05-geometry-settle.md` attempt 2; belongs in the next `/author-task` refresh.

And because *"the false red is gone"* and *"the guard still works"* are different claims (a vacuous guard also passes 2/2), the draining fire **re-ran the mutation control itself rather than inheriting it**: a temporary post-baseline palisade turned `:389` **red at Expected 94 / Received 97**, reverted, revert verified two ways. **F-1036-2's shipped-mutation defect did NOT recur** — checked by reading the whole 2-hunk diff, not by trusting the report.

It obeyed my scope-1 clause *"STOP-and-report only if the CLASS moved: a failure at a line other than `:233`"*: baselines ran **7/7, 7/7, then 6/7 with the failure at `:260`** — `repair interrupt does not debit and no-funds repair shows one blocked float` — and it halted invocations 4-6 as ordered. **But `:260` is not a different class. s1040 probed it and it is the BYTE-IDENTICAL assertion to `:233`:** `await expect.poll(() => page.evaluate(() => window.__THREE_GAME_DIAGNOSTICS__?.build.repair.progress ?? 0)).toBeGreaterThan(0)`, same fingerprint `Expected: > 0, Received: 0` after the 5s poll timeout. **The ~150 ms-window-vs-~100 ms-poller root cause (F-1040-1) explains BOTH tests**; the repair-interrupt test is simply the second site of one defect. **This is the F-1033-1 family repeating with a new face:** that finding taught *"stop clauses belong on the root-cause CLASS, never on a predicted pass/fail count"* — I honoured the letter (no count) and broke the spirit (**a line number is just as brittle a proxy for a class when the defect has more than one site**). **The run cost nothing and bought something:** it found the second site for free, which a `-g`-scoped run would never have surfaced. **REQUEUE OWED with a changed premise (§7.5)** — the refresh is small and the diagnosis is unchanged: re-key the stop clause to the **root-cause class** (*"any `build.repair.progress` `toBeGreaterThan(0)` poll returning 0"* — record the site and PROCEED, rather than halt), and **widen scope 3 to fix both sites** (`:233` and `:260`) since both are per-test `setBalance` windows on the same `Balance.wreck.repairSeconds`. Note `:262` already does `waitForSim(Balance.wreck.repairSeconds + 0.3)`, so **that test reads the balance value dynamically and will follow a per-test override correctly** — verified at file:line, not assumed. - ⚠️ **F-1041-1 (REAL, latent vacuous-guard trap — and it is a CORRECTION TO THE PREDECESSOR'S OWN REFRESH NOTE, caught only because s1041 re-probed a handed-down claim instead of pasting it).** F-1040-3 handed the next fire this line, in both the BACKLOG and the goal leaf: *"`:262` already calls `waitForSim(Balance.wreck.repairSeconds + 0.3)`, so it reads the balance dynamically and will follow a per-test override correctly — verified at file:line, not assumed."* **It is FALSE, and acting on it would have shipped a guard that cannot fail.** `setBalance` (`e2e/m2-05-base-damage-repair.spec.ts:41`) is **page-side** — it calls `window.__GR_TEST__.setBalance`. But the spec **also statically imports the balance table node-side** (`:4`, `import { Balance } from '../src/game/Balance'`), and `:262`'s `waitForSim(page, Balance.wreck.repairSeconds + 0.3)` is a **node-side read**: it keeps meaning **1.2 + 0.3 simulated seconds no matter what the page is told.** The two processes hold different copies of the module; a page-side override is **invisible to node**. **Consequence:** the refresh's own scope-3 (raise `wreck.repairSeconds` to `N` per-test to widen the `:260` observation window) would leave the `:252` test waiting ~1.5 sim seconds for a repair that now needs `N` — so `:263` (no `repair_palisade` sink logged) and `:264` (still wrecked) would pass **trivially even if the repair-interrupt were completely broken.** That is the **F-1026-1 vacuous-guard class reached by accident rather than intent**, which is the worse way to reach it, because nothing about the diff looks like a weakened assertion. **Fixed in the master before it ever ran:** one local const now feeds both the `setBalance` call and the `waitForSim` argument, and **scope 4 gains a second mutation control aimed exactly at this** — remove the interrupt so the repair completes; `:263`/`:264` **must** go red; if they stay green the wait is shorter than the dwell and the guard is vacuous. **Also verified while checking:** `:262` is the **ONLY** node-side read of `Balance.wreck.repairSeconds` in the file (`grep -n "Balance\."`), so the re-key is complete at that one line; and `waitForSim` (`:84-91`) waits in **simulated** seconds against `timeAlive` with a **15s real** timeout, so even `N=8` costs ~1.04s real at `timescale=8` — the re-key cannot blow the timeout. **LESSON (the third fire in a row saved by measuring instead of inheriting, and the first where the stale claim was itself a *verification claim*): "verified at file:line" in a predecessor's handoff is still a CLAIM. It carries a timestamp and an author, not a guarantee — Mistake #4 does not exempt sentences containing the word "verified". The re-probe cost two greps.**
- 🔁 **`lane-m2-05-repair-dwell-observable` REFRESHED + RE-QUEUED to lane-c (s1041, attempt 2 — changed premise per §7.5, an identical retry being forbidden).** Two changes, both owed by F-1040-3: **(1) the scope-1 stop clause is re-keyed from a LINE to the root-cause CLASS BY FINGERPRINT** — *any* `expect.poll(... build.repair.progress ...).toBeGreaterThan(0)` returning `Received: 0`, at **any** line in **any** test in the file, is IN CLASS → **record the site and PROCEED**; stop only on a received value other than `0`, a failure that is not a progress-poll, or a different spec file. **(2) scope 3 now repairs BOTH sites** (`:233` and `:260`), with the F-1041-1 trap written into it and a per-site mutation control in scope 4. Attempt 1's rate data is folded into the WHY as measured evidence: the class fails **~1 in 3 full-file desktop invocations and the SITE VARIES** — s1040's unmodified-main control failed at `:233`, attempt 1's baselines failed at `:260` with `:233` green 3/3 — while isolated runs are 3/3 green, which is why the six-separate-invocations full-file shape (F-1036-2) is mandatory and a `-g` run would hide the defect. **SAFE-DUPE re-verified fresh rather than inherited:** `git log main..lane/e2-arsenal` is **EMPTY** (attempt 1 reset the branch and committed nothing) and `git diff --stat main lane/e2-arsenal` shows only `STATUS.md`/`BACKLOG.md`/`goals.json` where the lane is *behind* main — **no `src/` or `e2e/` difference at all**, so nothing can be lost by the reset.

(1335/3053)`. **Neither touches bytes players receive**, so neither is owner-gated — unlike F-1051-2, which stays on the desk untouched. ➡️ ✅ **BOTH CLOSED s1053 `e7423018`.** The confirmation now reads `GR_PAGES_PRODUCTION_URL` (default `https://gold-rush-3in.pages.dev`) with a **bounded** 3-attempt / 15s retry that terminates by construction (`[ATTEMPT -eq 3] || sleep 15`, each fetch capped by `AbortSignal.timeout(20000)`; runner wall-clocks corroborate — stale 31s, unreachable 30s, retry 15s stopping at the first match), and the no-URL case now says `wrangler exited 0 but published no URL` in those words. **NOTE FOR THE NEXT READER: this is still UNEXERCISED against the real internet** — no deploy has succeeded since, so the alias path has been proven only against a local hermetic server. - ✅ **F-1039-2 CLOSED s1053 (stamps drift into the future) — and the fix is in `scripts/fire.md` §1.3, where the finding asked for it.** The rule is now: **derive stamps with `date "+%Y-%m-%dT%H:%MZ"`, never arithmetic.** Evidence that this was worth writing down: **s1053 itself hand-computed a `02:25Z` refresh stamp at `02:11` local — 14 minutes into the future — in the same fire that was reading the finding** (prior offenders: s1038 +46 min, s1046 +56/+50 min). ⚠️ **AND A TRAP WAS FOUND WHILE FIXING IT, so the fix is deliberately NOT `date -u` as the finding originally suggested:** the trailing `Z` is a **mislabel** (Bangkok, UTC+07: verified s1053, local `02:21` vs true UTC `19:21`) — **and the ground truth is worse than either side assumed, because I measured it instead of picking a side: THE CONVENTION IS INCONSISTENT ACROSS FIRES.** s1050/s1051/s1052 all stamped **local** (s1050's own text reads *"it is 00:56 local"* against a `00:56+07` commit), while s1047–s1049 are recorded as having switched to **true UTC** (`date -u`). **Two conventions ~7 h apart, both labeled `Z`**, on top of hand-computed errors of **+14 to +56 min**. ➡️ **So the fix is NOT "pick a timezone" — it is to stop trusting the stamp text at all.** `fire.md` §1.3 now **binds staleness judgements to the OWNING COMMIT** (`git log -1 --format=%cI` on the commit that wrote line-1), which is timezone-proof and arithmetic-proof, and **forbids taking over a lock on stamp text alone** — a ~7 h "gap" is far more likely a UTC-vs-local stamp than a dead fire. New stamps stay **local** for continuity with the three most recent fires; the label may only be corrected as a **flag-day**. **I CORRECTED MY OWN FIRST DRAFT OF THIS LAW MID-FIRE:** it originally asserted the local convention was consistent "for hundreds of sessions", which the s1047–s1049 evidence contradicts — the claim was retracted before the handoff rather than left standing.

## s1071 (2026-07-26, fire) — THE REFILL CAME BACK GREEN IN 14 MINUTES, AND BOTH "DEFECTS" TURNED OUT TO BE THE TEST LYING ABOUT THE ENGINE
- ✅ **DRAINED — `lane/m3` @ `bd1b0044` (`runner(lane-a): e1-briefing-truth-unmasked.md`, 09:23:11) merged at `43c38676`.** s1070 queued it at 09:09 and handed off at 09:16 predicting "drain it when it done-moves"; it done-moved and committed **while that handoff was being written**, so it was already waiting when I took the lock. **Verdict: both F-1068 reds were TEST-HARNESS artifacts. The engine was right both times, and the slice touches only `e2e/contract-briefings.spec.ts` — zero `src/`, zero `assets/`, no unlock rule, no contract loader.** Review `reviews/e1-briefing-truth.md`; 12 screenshots in `reviews/shots-e1-briefing-truth/`, regenerated by my own gate run in main's tree.
- 🔬 **F-1068-2 SOLVED, and the deciding line is the harness's — I re-derived it from source rather than accepting the runner's conclusion, because the master demanded exactly that.** `fallbackReason = 'debug-disabled'` is set at `src/meta/ContractFamilies.ts:1148` only when `requestedId && !debug && !launched && !liveSagaFlagship`. `launched` is `isPlayerContractLaunch()` (`:1208`), which is literally **`sessionStorage.getItem(PLAYER_CONTRACT_LAUNCH_KEY) === id`** (`:1210`); the key is written by `stagePlayerContractLaunch()` (`:1078`), whose caller `launchContract()` (`src/main.ts:213-221`) stages it and then calls **`window.location.reload()`** — a real navigation. The test's `seedProfile()` installed a Playwright `addInitScript` containing **`sessionStorage.clear()`**, and `addInitScript` re-runs on *every* navigation — so it wiped the launch key **on the post-click reload, before the app booted and read it**, manufacturing `launched === false` in the test and nowhere else. Deleting that one line is the whole repair. **The sibling helper `clearStorage()` (`:143`) correctly keeps its `sessionStorage.clear()`** — its tests go straight to `/?debug&contract=…`, where `debug` short-circuits the launch-key check. Not a Mistake #10 shape after all; the player was never affected.
- 🔬 **F-1068-3 SOLVED — the engine locked the card CORRECTLY and the fixture was under-seeded.** `e1-baron`'s rule is `science-complete+2-secured` (`src/meta/ContractUnlock.ts:48-54`), requiring science complete **and** `securedContracts.size >= 2` — *two distinct secured contract ids*. The fixture seeded exactly one (`the-claim`), so `data-contract-locked="true"` was the right answer. Seeding `e1-dry-gulch` as the second makes the fixture model a player who has genuinely earned the whole E1 board — **a precondition repair, not a bent expectation**: the unlock rule is untouched and the neighbouring rungs stay coherent (`e1-dry-gulch` = `wave10OnClaim`, `e1-twin-banks` = `firstSecuredClaim`, `e1-night-shift` = `science≥3`).
- 🧾 **EVIDENCE, real numbers: tsc clean · build green (`✓ 1.30s`) · own spec `--workers=1` **14/14 passed (1.6m) — 7/7 desktop-chrome + 7/7 mobile-chrome at 390px**, exactly the 7/7-both-projects s1070 predicted · both formerly-failing tests now green (`plain no-debug board launch…` 5.1s/4.8s; `board cards show the same briefing data` 5.0s/5.4s) · console + page errors **zero**, asserted in-suite at `:299-300` inside passing tests · adjacent `board-era-chapters` + `board-gating-and-profiles` + `board-upcoming-surveys` **12/12 GREEN** both projects.**
- ✅ **THE 061 REDS WERE PROVEN PRE-EXISTING, NOT INHERITED (Mistake #4).** `061-first-claim-onboarding` failed 6 tests alongside the graft, and s1068's F-1068-4 already called them known reds — but a carried claim is not evidence. ## s1072 (2026-07-26, fire) — THE UNOWNED ONBOARDING RED HAS A ROOT CAUSE, AND IT IS A SPAWN POINT THAT MOVED TWO UNITS SEVEN DAYS AGO
- 🔬 **F-1072-1 (ROOT-CAUSED from a dry board — F-1071-2 is no longer unowned).** `061-first-claim-onboarding` has been red since **2026-07-19**, and three fires carried it as *"unowned, wants an attended triage rung."* It is now explained end to end, by measurement rather than by reading. **Reproduced first:** `--project=desktop-chrome --workers=1` ⇒ **3 failed / 1 passed**, matching the s1068/s1071 fingerprint exactly; the failure is `expect(getByTestId('town-bark-speaker')).toHaveText('Marta Vale')` at `e2e/061-first-claim-onboarding.spec.ts:74`, **Received: `"Elder Rowan"`**. The single passing test is the only one that never calls `waitForFirstClaimGuide()`.
- 🎯 **THE MECHANISM: the greeter is chosen by PROXIMITY between exactly two candidates, and the player's spawn point moved across the midpoint.** `TownScene.firstClaimGreeter()` (`src/town/TownScene.ts:1293-1307`) iterates the roster and **skips every actor except `tavernkeeper` and `elder`**, returning whichever is nearest the hero — with no bark-radius gate, so it always returns one of the two. **`d1f549d5` (2026-07-19, `runner(lane-a): lane-landmark-collision.md`) changed `HERO_START` from `(0, 0.06, 0)` to `(0, 0.06, 2)`** — a two-unit shift made to fix a landmark collision, which crossed the midpoint between Marta Vale and Elder Rowan and **silently reassigned the onboarding greeting**. Distances computed both ways, and the flip is robust in both coordinate systems: at **z=0** Marta 8.64 / Rowan 9.91 ⇒ **Marta greets**; at **z=2** Marta 10.28 / Rowan 8.53 ⇒ **Rowan greets** (definition coords) — and at the plaza-placed RUNTIME coords the same flip holds (z=0: Marta 8.11 / Rowan 9.50; z=2: Marta 9.44 / Rowan 8.53).
- 🧾 **THREE CANDIDATE EXPLANATIONS WERE KILLED BY MEASUREMENT BEFORE THE RIGHT ONE SURVIVED, and each kill is worth recording.** **(1) "the town layout drifted"** — FALSE: both actors' `position` literals in `src/town/townsfolk.ts` are **byte-identical at every commit** from `0aace826` through HEAD. **(2) "the roster gating changed and the elder appeared"** — FALSE: `requiresBuilding` is **absent on both** at every one of those commits (it gates only `storekeeper`/`preacher`), so both have always been visible. **(3) "the townsfolk walk, so the winner is racy"** — FALSE: `TownActorRuntime.position` *is* live (`:2267`) and *is* time-driven for looping actors (`:2299`), but **`loop` is absent on both greeter candidates** (only `youngster_a`/`youngster_b`/`newsie`/`prospector` walk), so the selection is deterministic, not flaky.
- ⚠️ **METHOD WARNING, paid for in this fire: a `-S` pickaxe that says "never changed" is not evidence about a value that changed inside an unchanged line.** `git log -S "HERO_START = new THREE.Vector3"` reported **only** the 2026-07-07 creation and appeared to exonerate the spawn outright — because the pickaxe reports commits where the **count** of a string changes, and that literal occurs exactly once before and after. **`-G` (regex) found `d1f549d5` immediately.** The false exoneration cost most of an investigation and nearly shipped "cause unknown."
- ⚖️ **WHICH SIDE IS WRONG — the ENGINE IS COHERENT AND THE ASSERTION OVER-SPECIFIES, but the flip was an ACCIDENT, and those two facts point at different fixes.** For the engine: the two-candidate whitelist is deliberate and **unchanged since the feature shipped**, and the greeting line itself — `FIRST_CLAIM_GREETING` (`:111`) = *"The valley's open. **The tavern keeps the contracts.** Go stake your first claim."* — is written in the **third person about the tavern**, which reads correctly from the schoolhouse elder and distinctly oddly from the tavernkeeper standing inside her own tavern. The guide trail still targets the tavernkeeper (`:1240`), so the chain *elder greets → trail → tavern → board → launch* is coherent. Against: `d1f549d5` was a **landmark-collision fix** with no stated intent to touch onboarding, so today's greeter is **accidental, not designed** — and relaxing the test would bless an accident. **The spec has been modified exactly once, at its introduction, so the assertion is original and was correct on the day it was written.**
- 🛑 **NO CORRECTIVE AUTHORED, and the reason is §7.3 rather than §2E's hard limit.** Both repairs are one-liners and neither is a fire's call: **(a)** relax `:74` to accept either whitelisted greeter — matches the engine's ratified contract, but ratifies a side effect; **(b)** pin `firstClaimGreeter()` to `tavernkeeper` (or nudge `HERO_START` back), restoring the greeter the feature shipped with. **This is a design fork about who welcomes a brand-new player on the E1 release path, so it goes to the desk with a recommendation instead of into a lane.** Zero console/page errors throughout: nothing is crashing, it is a question of whose voice the onboarding speaks in.

🔴 **F-1079-2/3/4 — THREE REAL DEFECTS IN THE NEW ENDPOINT, ALL QUEUED AS `tasks/redeem-harden.md` (main slot). NONE IS REACHABLE TODAY — the endpoint 503s without a KV binding and no code exists until the owner mints one — but ALL THREE WANT LANDING BEFORE THE FIRST REAL STUB IS ISSUED.** · **F-1079-2:** `redeem.ts:33` is `return mint(...)` inside a `try`; in an async function a plain `return` of a promise settles *after* the try scope exits, so the `catch` never runs — **verified by executing both forms**, not by reading. A malformed authenticated mint 500s instead of returning `400 bad_payload`. · **F-1079-3 (the one that can hurt somebody):** `kv.delete` runs *before* the client persists, so a lost response or a quota failure **permanently kills the code** — every retry reads `bad_stub`, and the pre-check probes the *selected-skin* key rather than the *owned-list* key that actually fails. Fix is idempotence, not tighter single-use: the grant is client-side `localStorage` anyway, so single-use enforces nothing while costing a real prize. · **F-1079-4:** every sibling public write gates on `bumpCounter`/`clientIpHash`; `redeem.ts` copied their CORS block **verbatim and dropped the rate limit**, leaving unbounded unauthenticated billed KV reads. CORS is not a defence — a non-browser client sending no `Origin` passes. **Brute force is NOT the risk:** `randomBytes(12)` is 96 bits and the pattern matches it exactly.

CODEX: model=gpt-5.6-sol effort=high`, where the `CODEX:` sits **mid-line and therefore invisible to `^CODEX:`**. ➡️ **ROOT CAUSE, and it is a documentation hole rather than anyone's mistake:** ✓ `.claude/skills/author-task/SKILL.md`'s mandatory skeleton (`:14-20`) **contains no `CODEX:` line at all**, and ✓ the string appears **nowhere** in `.claude/`, `docs/`, or `AGENTS.md` — so authors have been improvising it onto the role line, in the one position the parser cannot see. **The older 262 predate the one-line role style and got it right by accident of layout.** ⚠️ **Consequence to be honest about: this is a mechanism the factory believed it had. Writes under `.claude/` are gated for fires ⇒ **OWED to attended (or one owner word to let a fire write `.claude/`).** Until it lands, **any author — fire or attended — must put the `CODEX:` line on its own line, flush left**, or the master gets `medium` no matter what it says.

🔴 **F-1083-1 — THE STORAGE GUARD IS UNREUSABLE BY CONSTRUCTION, WHICH IS *WHY* THIS THREAD HAS PLAYED THREE ROUNDS OF WHACK-A-MOLE.** ✓ VERIFIED s1083 by reading the file. `rawGet`/`rawSet` (`src/game/ProfileStorage.ts:484,492`) are declared plain `function` and are **NOT exported**, so the other **20 files** holding direct storage calls *cannot* reuse the guard even if they wanted to — every future fix is structurally forced to be another local `try`/`catch`. Measured denominator: `grep -rn "storage\.getItem\|storage\.setItem\|localStorage\.getItem\|localStorage\.setItem" src/` = **59 direct calls across 21 files**; this drain guards **three**. This is the exact shape of F-1080-1 (`bumpCounter`/`clientIpHash` copied four times because it was module-private) — the second time this week that a *private* helper has manufactured duplicate work. Codex's boot-path subset, with call chains: `accountSync.install → readSession` · `hasLegacyProfileData` / `migrateLegacySuspendResources` · `StartMenu.render → readSaveSlots` · `reconcileActiveEpoch` · `loadResearchState → loadMetaProgress` · `E7SignalSystem → readMilestones` · `readTownName`. ➡️ **AUTHORED s1083 as `tasks/lane-blocked-storage-boot-3.md` (goal leaf `rf-22`), queued to lane-a**: export ONE guarded accessor, re-point those named boot-path sites, leave the other ~26 alone. The predecessor master deferred this as "a design decision"; it is a *technical* strategy choice with no player-visible behaviour change and no canon surface, so it is fire-authorable — but its firewall must **name every file it may touch and lift the predecessor's blanket `NO: any other file under src/`**, or the runner will lawfully STOP again (F-1082-1, twice-proven).

🔴 **F-1083-2 — `e2e/tile-identity-pass.spec.ts` IS A GATE THAT CANNOT BE PASSED, AND IT HAS NOW STOPPED AN HONEST LANE.** ✓ VERIFIED s1083 by three runs, load held constant. Clean main **twice** = 1 passed / **3 failed**, identical identities (`:81` desktop, `:57` mobile, `:81` mobile); the grafted tree = the same 1/3, same identities ⇒ pre-existing, and the storage drain neither causes nor cures it. **Two of the four tests are non-deterministic in the strict sense** — `:57` mobile compares a fingerprint against a second render *of the same seed* and gets a different hash (`04454b25a520ff9618d1ac4c8eaae0251053434a0d40cc2be0235044684618f7` vs `d374cf2c0966e55d7960ee84fd6a536af5987deb4ccce927f67cce0772185a5a`) — and the rest flood `THREE.GLTFLoader: Couldn't load texture blob:…` console errors. ⚠️ **It is also LOAD-SENSITIVE: the lane reported `2/4` inside a 7-suite battery while both isolated clean-main controls give `1/4`.** A suite whose pass count moves with ambient load is already telling you its determinism is broken. **This is a standing tax: `lane-blocked-storage-boot-2` declined to claim READY-FOR-GATES purely because of it, costing a re-verification cycle.** ➡️ **ATTENDED/OWNER:** either repair the determinism or mark those two known-red with a fingerprint — but do not leave it as a silent blocker on every future lane. **No corrective queued: diagnosing renderer determinism is not fire-authorable.**

🟡 **F-1083-3 — DEFERRED PERSISTENCE-POLICY EDGE IN THE NEW FALLBACK (non-blocking, recorded so it is not lost).** Raised by the lane's own independent review: if a read fails *transiently* and a later write succeeds, the cached empty snapshot can overwrite tile state that was never seen. The master explicitly required caching the empty fallback and **forbade** retries and shadow storage, so this is a policy decision, not a defect in the delivered scope. It only bites a browser that throws once and then recovers, which is not a mode any named target exhibits (Safari's block is a standing setting, not a flap). **Recorded, not queued.**

🟡 **F-1083-5 — "NO READY MASTER EXISTS" IS NOW A *MEASURED* CLAIM, NOT AN INHERITED ONE, AND HERE IS THE MEASUREMENT SO NO FIRE REPEATS IT.** ✓ VERIFIED s1083. That 126 is **NOT a queue of ready work** — it is mostly an artefact of the runner's renaming (a master ships under a differently-named done-move, F-1064-2), and BACKLOG spot-checks of the five most recent, BACKLOG-mentioned candidates (`lane-c-ed-04-gizmo-guards-vacuous`, `lane-a-perf-05-startup-attribution`, `lane-m1-m2-resource-guards`, `lane-resource-timing-false-greens`, `lane-m2-05-repair-dwell-observable`) found **every one already queued and run** in a prior fire, several with recorded no-ops and correctives. ➡️ **Therefore: refills for lane-b/c/d/art must be AUTHORED, and a name-based sweep of `tasks/` is a false lead — do not treat the 126 as a ladder.** Establishing shipped-ness for a candidate requires a **content probe against main** per candidate (F-1063-1), which is a multi-fire audit, not a refill step. ⚠️ **And blind-queueing one of them is Mistake #8, the 824k-token flail.** The cheap, correct move for an idle fire remains: author ONE master from a spec slice plus its evidence chain, which is what this fire did (`rf-22`).

The Completeness Law says the ledger is the thing the dashboard renders and that an item missing here is invisible; a handoff line scrolls out of reach in a few fires. **Both F-1082-3 and F-1082-4 are live owner-desk items** and are carried forward in this fire's handoff. ➡️ **Standing reminder for every fire: a finding written only into line-1 is not recorded.**

🔴 **F-1082-2 — THE BLOCKED-STORAGE BOOT HAS A *THIRD* UNGUARDED READ, AND IT IS THE SAME ASYMMETRY A SECOND TIME.** ✓ VERIFIED s1082 by reading the files, not by inheriting the run report. `src/game/TileStateStore.ts:51` is `const raw = this.storage.getItem(tileStateKey(...))` — a **direct** call on a `ProfileStorage`, which `ProfileStorage.ts:92` defines as `Pick<Storage,'getItem'|'setItem'|'removeItem'>`, i.e. the raw browser API. Under a throwing `getItem` it propagates, and `src/systems/E6TileConsumerSystem.ts:380` (`readState()`) calls it on the boot path via `new E6TileConsumerSystem → Game → startGame`. **The striking part: the SAME CLASS already guards its WRITE** — `commitAtRunEnd():99` wraps `this.storage.setItem` in `try`/`catch` with a `console.warn`. The read was simply forgotten. That is the *identical* asymmetry as `ProfileStorage`'s guarded `browserStorage():476-482` beside its (formerly) unguarded `rawGet():484-486` — **the same defect shape, in two different files, both on the same boot path.** The fix is provably safe rather than invented: `parseSnapshot(null)` already returns `emptySnapshot()` (`:172-173`), so "storage threw" collapses to "no tile state saved yet", the ordinary first-boot state every caller handles. ➡️ Queued to lane-a as `lane-blocked-storage-boot-2.md` (goal leaf `rf-20`). ⚠️ **Wider than the fix: `grep -rn "storage\.getItem\|localStorage\.getItem\|sessionStorage\.getItem" src/` returns ~33 direct reads** (`SaveSlots`, `MetaProgress`, `Medals`, `ResearchTree`, `AccountSync`, `RideTogether`, `TownNaming`, `encyclopedia/*`, `Game.ts:6168`, …). The task **reports** which are boot-path rather than guarding them — choosing a migration strategy for 33 call sites is a design decision, not a lane fix. **That triage is the next rung, and it is the real end of this thread; guarding them one finding at a time is exactly the whack-a-mole this has already played three rounds of.**

🟡 **F-1082-1 — THE `lane-blocked-storage-boot` MASTER CONTRADICTED ITSELF, AND CODEX DID THE RIGHT THING BY STOPPING.** ✓ VERIFIED s1082 by reading the master beside the run log. Scope item 3 ordered: *"If you find a third unguarded storage touch on the boot path, **guard it** and say so in your report"* — while the Firewall ordered: *"**NO:** any other file under `src/` … **at all**"*. Those are mutually exclusive the moment the third touch lands outside `ProfileStorage.ts`, which is exactly what happened (`TileStateStore.ts`, F-1082-2). Codex resolved the contradiction **in favour of the firewall** and STOPPED with a precise escalation naming the file it needed authorized. **That is the behaviour CLAUDE.md §4.5 asks for** ("Codex reporting adjacent problems = good; fixing out of scope = violation") and it cost one run of ~120k tokens rather than an out-of-scope edit nobody had gated. ➡️ **Authoring rule, for the next master anyone writes: a scope item that says "and fix it if you find it" MUST name the files it is pre-authorizing, or the firewall must carry a conditional lift clause.** A blanket `NO: any other file under src/` silently outranks every "if you find X, fix it" in the scope above it. The continuation master `lane-blocked-storage-boot-2.md` carries an explicit **🔓 FIREWALL LIFT** line for one named file, which is the shape to copy. **No corrective task needed against Codex — the run is a success story with a misleading exit label.**

🔴 **F-1081-10 — THE DEPLOY FAILURE IS A REPRODUCIBLE *TRANSPORT* FAILURE, NOT AUTH AND NOT SIZE. THE STANDING DIAGNOSIS IS WRONG, AND THIS NOW BLOCKS SIX MERGED GAMEPLAY CHANGES.** ✓ VERIFIED s1081 by running `bash scripts/deploy.sh` **twice** and reading the wrangler log both times — the first fresh attempt since s1078, because re-running a known-failing command is only pointless if you don't read the log. Both attempts fail identically at `pages deploy`, exit 0 (the script never blocks, as designed), with `✘ Failed to upload files. Please try again. Error: {}` — an **empty** error object, which is why this has been mis-attributed. The log tells the real story: repeated `TypeError: fetch failed` whose `[cause]` is **`Error: write EPIPE`** and **`SocketError: other side closed`**, across many parallel upload connections, fatal after ~30 s.

**What this rules OUT, with evidence:**
- **NOT auth** — zero `401`/`403`/`token`/`scope` anywhere in the log; wrangler authenticates and reaches the API fine.
- **NOT quota/rate** — no `429`.
- **NOT the ~118 MB size theory** (s1078's premise): the script's own budget check **passes with room to spare** — `16,480,199 bytes` against the cap, **8,519,801 bytes of headroom**, on both desktop and mobile.
- **NOT a one-off transient** — the retry reproduced it exactly, so "Please try again" is not the answer either. (And bandwidth is not the constraint: F-1076-1 measured a **184.9 MB** git push completing in **65 s** on this machine.)

**What it points AT:** the connection is being closed mid-write by the far side, repeatedly, under wrangler's *parallel* upload fan-out. Candidate causes worth one owner-side look each: wrangler 4.107.0's upload concurrency against this network path (a proxy, a router, or an ISP dropping many simultaneous long-lived POSTs), or a Cloudflare-side per-connection limit. ➡️ **CHEAP THINGS TO TRY BEFORE ANY BIG DECISION:** (a) a concurrency/`--no-bundle`-style throttle on the Pages upload, (b) the same deploy from a different network, (c) a wrangler version change. **`scripts/deploy.sh` also has no retry and no upload-concurrency control at all** — adding both is small, fire-authorable, and testable only by an owner-present run.
⚠️ **This supersedes the framing of F-1051-3 "option (2)".** The owner may have been offered the wrong choice: the blocker looks like a fixable transport setting, not something requiring the option-(2) tradeoff. **Six merged gameplay changes are now unplayable by the family** (`d2279d32`, `5bad5def`, `5b61349e`, `52fa15ad`, `29625771`, and today's `eb7278ac` + `925a0c3b`), which makes this the highest-value owner item on the desk.

F-1080-E, F-1054-1): **classify EVERY file the lane touched, and say how many you looked at.** A false-ahead verdict is only as wide as its denominator.

<br>🔴 **F-1101-1 (NEW, s1101 — THE WHOLE-SUITE GATE POISONS ITSELF; THIS IS THE GENERAL CASE OF THE F-1084-1 / F-1095-2 "LOAD-SENSITIVE" CLASS).** `playwright.config.ts` pins no `workers`, so `npm test` defaults to cores/2 = **8 workers**, each a full three.js/WebGL Chrome. Measured on the 16-core Mac: **loadavg 34.16, 71 chromium procs**, per-test durations inflating 2–13 s → 40–60 s with strikes at exactly the 30 s ceiling, and reds **accelerating** 3 (t+1 min) → 37 of 143 (t+6 min, ~26%). **Control that makes it a fact:** machine quiesced (load 35.76 → 10.22, zero leftover chromium), then `051-audio-governor` re-run `--workers=1` → **4/4 PASS in 3.2–3.6 s**, including the two that had just failed at 11.3 s and 10.4 s. A `--workers=4` rerun independently corroborates (audio family absent from its reds). **Why it stayed invisible:** drains run small batteries that never cross the contention threshold — it only bites at whole-suite scale, i.e. exactly the thing s1095 asked for and nobody had done. **Blast radius:** the first fire to run the suite and trust the number publishes ~37 phantom regressions AND buries the real ones (F-1101-2) inside them. s1100 died 11 min into that exact run; s1101 caught it only by controlling. **OWED (not taken as a drive-by — this config gates every drain):** an authored master pinning `workers` explicitly with this finding named in the comment. Three of five E1 release maps could be **won by standing still in the deep river** (The Claim secured wave 10 in 77 s with 0 kills / 0 damage / 0 gold / 0 buildings), because enemies never enter deep water and `endRun()` is reached only by hero death — so the "Wet powder" disarm price bought the only thing that could lose the run. **Merge classification was trivial and verified, not assumed:** merge-base `ab7cba01`, `git diff ab7cba01 main` over all three files **EMPTY** ⇒ all three **LANE-TOUCHED-ONLY**, no 3-way, applied byte-identical to the lane tip. `lane/m3`'s other four commits are false-ahead and were **not** drained.

With the datum false the hero **cannot stand on a deep sample by walking**, so every deleted assertion (wade-speed sampling, the disarm, the re-arm) tests a code path that no longer exists — they would fail, not pass. The replacement is **stronger on the changed behaviour**: `riverSamples === 0`, `zone === 'shallows'`, `terrainSample(-12,0)` → `{walkable:false, zone:'river', waterClass:'deep'}`, `disarmed === false` (the player **keeps** the weapons the old design traded away), and it still spawns an enemy to prove bolts fly. `gt-05` moves in lockstep and stays regression-sensitive (`river: {walkable:false, speedMul:0}`), so a silent revert of the datum fails it.

Deliverable: one added test proving no browser is launched on the unset branch, plus the usual mutation control aimed at *that* branch. 🔵 **F-1089-3 QUEUED AS rf-29 — THE LAST HARDCODED PORT (s1090, FIRE-AUTHORED: `tasks/lane-probe-boot-ownership.md`, goal leaf `rf-29-probe-boot-ownership`, queued to lane-c).** Third and last known instance of the class rf-27 cured (`a9fccf75`) and rf-28 guarded (`8fb97691`). ✓ VERIFIED s1090 by reading the file, not by inheriting the report: `scripts/probe-plain-boot-console.mjs:23` is `const baseURL = process.argv[2] ?? 'http://127.0.0.1:5188';` — it then opens a browser and prints console/page errors **as drain evidence**, with **no ownership check at all**. An unattended run therefore reports a clean-or-dirty boot for **whatever tree happens to be serving 5188**, and a drain quotes it. **The task reuses the shipped `resolveBase` rather than writing a second `lsof` parser** (a duplicate would make this the third copy of that parsing, which is how the class spread in the first place), orders resolution **before** `chromium.launch()` so an unset base cannot leak a browser, and — the non-obvious ruling — **rejects a positional `argv[2]` loudly instead of ignoring it**: s1090 grepped the whole repo and found **no automated caller** (no npm script, no CI), but **two shipped masters** (`lane-blocked-storage-boot-3.md:58`, `lane-blocked-storage-access-throw.md:75`) invoke it positionally as a drain self-check, so the next author will copy that line out of habit — under a silent env-var switch their URL would be ignored and the probe would quietly measure 5188, making the *fix* a fresh instance of the *defect*. Guard: two subprocess tests wired into `test:node-guards` (expect `node --test` phase **54 → 56**), plus a mandatory mutation control that restores the 5188 default and is **watched to go RED** (rf-28's lesson: aim the control at the branch nobody has watched fail).

Re-probe each candidate’s CLOSURE by content before authoring from it.** This fire came within one probe of authoring an already-shipped refactor for the second time in the repo’s history (Mistake #8 shape).
