🟠 **F-1093-5 — MY OWN PRE-FLIGHT WAS MIS-ORDERED, AND rf-32 ATTEMPT 1 CORRECTLY STOPPED ON IT. FIXED AND RE-QUEUED WITH A NAMED LIFT (s1093).** The rf-32 master I authored this fire listed four pre-flight checks to run **before** `git checkout -B lane/perf main` — but checks 2–4 are premises about **main’s** content, and `lane/perf` is **91 commits behind main**. So Codex ran check 3 (`grep -n "export function rawGet" src/game/ProfileStorage.ts` → must print `500`) against the **stale lane**, where that file still carries the *old private* `function rawGet` at **`:484`** — rf-22’s export does not exist there. The grep returned nothing, and the master said STOP if it did. ✅ **The runner did exactly the right thing: it stopped before the reset, changed no file and no git state, and said why** (`BLOCKED by mandatory preflight #3 … main has it, but the task forbids resetting unless all checks pass`). Its worktree is ✓ CLEAN and `lane/perf` is untouched at `da0f4240`. **A firewall STOP is success, not a failure — and this one was pointing at an authoring defect, not a stale premise.** ➡️ **FIX (not a blind re-queue): the pre-flight is now three ordered STEPS — (1) lane-safety `git log main..lane/perf` on the branch as it stands, (2) `git checkout -B lane/perf main`, (3) the three premise checks on the FRESHLY-RESET tree**, with an explicit tell written in: *“if you see a bare `function rawGet` at `:484` with no `export`, you are on the stale lane — you skipped STEP 2.”* Re-queued to lane-d, sha256-verified, `^CODEX:` at column 0. 📌 **The general lesson, worth more than the fix: a pre-flight check that describes MAIN must either run after the reset or be written to interrogate main explicitly (`git show main:<path>`). On a lane that is any distance behind, a bare `grep <path>` measures the stale tree and STOPs the run for the wrong reason.** s1092’s rf-31 master avoided this by writing “**on main**” into each of its checks — the same instinct, made explicit. This is now the third distinct way a lane’s staleness has produced a misleading signal this week (after false-ahead counts and stale-base phantom deletions), and unlike those two it cost a real Codex cycle.

🟢 **F-1102-1 — F-1101-2 IS ROOT-CAUSED AND QUEUED AS `078-focus-and-ledger-reverts` (lane-c). s1101 guessed "same family ⇒ likely one cause, not three"; the truth is THREE REVERTED FIXES ACROSS TWO COMMITS, and that guess would have sent a fixer hunting one imaginary shared cause.** ✓ VERIFIED s1102 by reading each diff, **with no test run at all** — s1101 left a whole-suite baseline running detached (pid 80822) and any control would have contaminated it (its own §D admits exactly that self-contamination). `e2e/078-ux-hygiene.spec.ts` has **one** commit in its life (`cd76c7f0`, 2026-07-10) and never moved again; the product moved on 2026-07-11 and took three fixes down with it. **(1) `:151`** — `93632a94` (audio MU-02 wave 1) collapsed `TownScene.openBoard`'s two-query `??` into ONE grouped `querySelector`, which returns first-in-**document-order** across the whole group, so the header's `data-contract-close` beats every contract card and Launch is never focused; that hunk deleted **verbatim the entire product change of `cd76c7f0`**, the commit that created this spec. **(2) `:130`** — `9b567437` (en-03 epoch pages) deleted `currentRestoreFocus`, the close-time focus restore, the `Tab` branch and `trapLedgerFocus()` from `src/encyclopedia/reader.ts` (−34 lines), reverting `acf1c476`. **(3) `:111`** — **the same commit `9b567437`** reverted `discoverLedgerEntry` from the unfiltered `readStoredDiscoveryValues` back to the filtering `readStoredDiscovered`, so an unknown id is dropped on read and the write-back yields `['baron']` instead of `['future-ledger-id','baron']` — undoing `e1f243f4`. **All three reproduce at BOTH `--workers=8` and `--workers=4`, so they are NOT F-1101-1 contention artefacts**; `:111` failed in 1.5 s in both runs. The master carries **RULING 3** (KEEP the newer unconditional `stopPropagation()` at `reader.ts:334` — it post-dates `acf1c476` and has a deliberate multiplayer rationale, so a literal revert-of-the-revert would regress multiplayer input) and **RULING 6** (the `aria-modal`/`inert` alternative to a JS focus trap is a product decision → **OWNER-GATED, flagged not implemented**).

🟢 **F-1103-1 — `--workers=4` WAS NEVER A QUIESCED INSTRUMENT EITHER; THE BASELINE WAS MEASURING A POISONED BOX WHILE EVERYONE ASSUMED IT WAS THE CURE. Baseline STOPPED, F-1101-1 master AUTHORED as a calibration.** ✓ VERIFIED s1103 by measuring the live box rather than reasoning about it: during s1101's detached `--workers=4` run the machine read **loadavg 34.35 / 38 chromium procs / 6 playwright workers** — statistically the *same* saturation s1101 recorded at `--workers=8` (**34.16**), and nothing like the **16–18** s1102 believed it to be. **That single measurement explains the anomaly nobody could place:** halving 8 → 4 barely moved the red rate (25.9% → 21.8% → **23.4%** at stop) because *both* counts saturate the box. Likely mechanism: each headless WebGL Chrome consumes roughly 4 cores, so 4 workers already ≈ 16 cores = the whole machine. ⇒ **s1102's datum (D) reading (i) is the right one, and "just pin 4" would have shipped a number that is still wrong.** **THE RUN WAS STOPPED** at **428/2378** (328 ✓ / 100 ✘, 18% of the suite) after a tree-scoped kill of pid 80822's 26 processes, with an explicit codex/lane safety check in the killer so no other slot could be caught. Justification, all measured: it had **~3.2 h left** at 10.3 tests/min (≈38 fires holding the box); codex was concurrently running full 078 gate batteries into the same machine, which is **exactly the self-contamination s1101's own §D admits ruined its previous rerun**; and both prior fires had already written that its number must not be quoted as a contention ratio. Cost, stated honestly: the remaining 1950 results, and the end-of-run JSON — **which never existed on disk at any point**, because playwright's json reporter only emits at run end (`logs/suite-runs/*.json` was referenced by `CURRENT-RUN.txt` but was never written). **Payoff, measured immediately after: loadavg 34.35 → 2.79, chromium 38 → 2**, so codex's remaining 078 gates ran honestly instead of poisoned. ➡️ **F-1101-1's master now exists: `tasks/lane-calibrate-suite-workers.md` → lane-d** (`1bcaf7bf`, goal leaf same commit), written as s1102 asked — a **calibration, not a blind pin**, explicitly permitted to land `workers: 1`. Its instrument is a 12-file subset frozen from the banked w4 log by a stated rule (fully green, ≥2 tests, slowest-first: green so failures cannot confound the duration signal, slowest so the files are the most contention-*sensitive* in the suite); **12 files because playwright parallelises across FILES, not tests** — a smaller subset cannot engage 8 workers and would silently measure nothing; runs at 1,2,4,8,**1 again** as a drift control that *voids the table* if the two references disagree >15%; and a decision rule fixed **in advance** (largest N with p95 duration inflation ≤1.25, no test >25 s, zero reds) so the outcome cannot be rationalised after the fact. `CURRENT-RUN.txt` now records the stop and its reasons for whoever restarts a suite run next.

🟠 **F-1103-2 — THE RETENTION LAW'S BIGGEST HOLE IS `.gitignore` LINE 7, AND IT IS 12× THE ART ONE. ⚠️ OWNER'S DESK — do not "fix" this unilaterally, it is a repo-size decision.** ✓ VERIFIED s1103 by counting, after noticing that s1101 recorded its contended suite log as "retained per RETENTION LAW" when it was **not retained in any sense that survives this disk**. `.gitignore:7` is a bare **`*.log`**, which silently swallows every factory log in the repo: **`tasks/runs/` holds 81 run logs / 202.6 MB with ZERO tracked**, and `logs/runs-archive/` — the mirror directory the RETENTION LAW names by name — contains exactly **1 tracked file**. So the law's "mirror into git before any hygiene pass" duty is, in practice, undone: s1033 correctly removed the `find -delete` prunes so the logs are safe from *deletion*, but they remain safe only on **this one disk**. This is the same class as F-1045-1 (art staging, 10 files / 16.5 MB in no commit) and **12× larger by bytes**; Mistake #11's question — "what dies with this disk today?" — currently answers *202.6 MB of factory history*. ⚠️ **AND IT IS INVISIBLE TO EVERY AUDIT THIS FACTORY HAS BUILT, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING.** F-1055-2's measurement — `git rev-list --objects --all --not --remotes` — enumerates blobs that **are in git but on no origin ref**. A gitignored file is not a blob at all, so that traversal **cannot see these 202.6 MB by construction**, and neither can `art-staging-audit.mjs`, whose LOCAL-ONLY bucket is defined the same way. So the two findings are **not duplicates and neither supersedes the other**: F-1055-2 asks *"which of my objects are only here?"* while F-1103-2 asks *"which of my bytes never became objects?"* — and Mistake #11 asks the union, which nothing currently measures. This is the **third** recurrence of the exact defect shape F-1054-1 and F-1055-1 were each fixed for (*the headline is narrower than the question printed above it*), and the ART-SLOT LAW's own words apply: **"If you find a third such gap, fix the class, not the instance."** The class fix is an audit that starts from **the filesystem** and subtracts what git knows, rather than starting from git's index and subtracting origin. **What s1103 DID do (proportionate, ~110 KB):** force-added the two whole-suite logs that are live evidence for F-1101-1 (`d318c3fc`). **What s1103 deliberately did NOT do:** commit 202.6 MB, which would inflate every future clone permanently and is a structural call that belongs to Robin, not a fire. ➡️ **RECOMMENDATION for the owner (one word each): (a) narrow `.gitignore:7` from `*.log` to the specific noisy paths so factory logs stop being swallowed by default; (b) for the 202.6 MB backlog, either gzip-mirror into `logs/runs-archive/` (logs compress ~10–20×, so ~10–20 MB — the cheap option, and the one s1103 recommends), or push them to a separate archive branch/remote that clones do not fetch, or accept disk-only retention and say so in the law so no future fire records "retained" for a file that is not.** Until Robin rules, **no fire should either commit the backlog or delete any of it.**

re-measuring the list found THREE** (`grep -rn data-contract-art-key e2e/` = 3 hits / 3 files): `town-t3-board:203` (expects `contract.artKey`), plus **`e2-pressure-garden:73` and `e2-trestle:73`, both expecting `contract-the-claim` and both red since 2026-07-20 unreported** — the standing "re-measure a finding's LIST" law paying out again (s1092 five→four, s1095 four→six). **`tsc` could never have caught it: `BOARD_CONTRACTS` (`town-t3-board:25-30`) is a spec-LOCAL literal, not an import from `src/`,** so its `artKey` field is a private frozen copy that no type-check reaches. ⚠️ **Repairing the renderer instead would REVERSE a ratified owner ruling — the master firewalls `src/**` and orders STOP-and-report if the runner disagrees (§7.3).** **AND THE FIX WAS RE-MEASURED, NOT JUST THE FINDING — which cut a scope item rather than adding one.** The constant assertion distinguishes no card from any other, so s1105 drafted a per-card **image-src** guard over the real fallback chain (`plate-contract-<id>` → interim board-card → `plate-contract-the-claim`), then **found `e2e/board-card-images.spec.ts` already does it and does it better**: it walks **all 10 epochs and asserts all 41 contracts**, checks every non-`the-claim` card's `img` src **differs from `the-claim`'s**, pins `e2-trestle` to `/e2-trestle/`, and requires zero console errors. **Scope item 3 therefore now FORBIDS duplicating that coverage** (it orders a pointer-comment instead), and `board-card-images.spec.ts` rides the battery as the adjacency whose greenness proves the DOM was right all along. 🔬 **F-1104-2 — TWO 8-DAY-DEAD PLAYWRIGHT CORPSES WERE ABOUT TO STOP F-1101-1's CALIBRATION *FOREVER*, BECAUSE THE GATE TESTED FOR EXISTENCE INSTEAD OF LIFE. REAPED.** ✓ VERIFIED s1104 by two CPU samples. The `20260727-044706` calibration dispatch STOPped **correctly** at its quiescence gate, reporting "two foreign Playwright processes" — but those processes were pids **24303/24384**, `npm exec playwright test --config playwright.accounts.config.ts` out of `worktrees/lane-b`, started **Sat Jul 18 09:49:38** (8 d 19 h), reparented to **`ppid 1`**, holding **0:00.26 and 0:00.54 total CPU with ZERO growth across 45 s.** Corpses, not work. Since the gate asks only *does a playwright process exist*, they would have STOPped this calibration **on every future dispatch, indefinitely** — burning ~79 k tokens per lawful STOP while the finding they block (the config line that poisons **every drain on the board**) stayed unmeasured. Reaped with SIGTERM behind an identity check (accounts-config playwright only; abort on any codex/runner/claude match); **0 playwright processes remain.** This is the exact recurrence the standing note *"accounts playwright + resident claude = attended — but CHECK CPU"* exists for, and s1070 saw these same corpses at 8 d and left them. **Class fix landed in the master, not just the instance:** `tasks/lane-calibrate-suite-workers.md` §4 now (a) drops `pgrep -fc`, which **macOS does not support** — it printed usage instead of a count, so the Chrome census was junk (**F-1104-4**, caught from the run log before it could misfire) — for a portable `ps`+`grep -c`, and (b) redefines "foreign process" as a **LIVE** one, ordering two samples ≥45 s apart and *discount the ones whose CPU has not moved*. Live processes still mean STOP; you still never kill what you did not start.

🟠 **F-1104-3 — `en-02-e1-coverage:244` IS A FLAKE ON BOTH TREES, AND ONE SAMPLE SAID "REGRESSION". THE REPEAT IS THE WHOLE FINDING.** ✓ VERIFIED s1104. First control read like a clean regression — **failed on the merged tree, passed on clean main** — which is exactly the shape that gets a good merge reverted. Repeating it disproved that: merged **1 pass / 1 fail**, control **1 pass / 1 fail**, identical. The failure is **never in a ledger assertion**; it is in the `approachSchoolhouse` helper, where `hold(page,'KeyA',1150)` and `hold(page,'KeyS',900)` are **wall-clock** holds whose distance travelled depends on how many frames the box renders in that window — so the hero simply arrives late and `activePrompt` never reads `schoolhouse`. That is **F-1101-1's load-sensitivity class in a helper**, and it is a standing false-red generator for every drain that touches the encyclopedia. **OWED (small, fire-authorable):** make the approach helpers poll-to-arrival rather than hold-for-a-duration. **Method note worth keeping: a fingerprint control run ONCE is a coin toss; the law's "fingerprint-matched with proof" means the control must be REPEATED before it can exonerate or convict.**

🔴 **F-1104-5 — A TASK MASTER'S SELF-CHECK NAMED TWO SPEC FILES THAT HAVE NEVER EXISTED, AND PLAYWRIGHT REPORTED GREEN ANYWAY.** ✓ VERIFIED s1104 by `ls`. Step 5 of `tasks/lane-078-focus-and-ledger-reverts.md` ordered the adjacent run as `e2e/en-01-ledger.spec.ts e2e/en-02-ledger.spec.ts e2e/en-03-epoch-pages.spec.ts`; the real files are **`en-01-claim-ledger.spec.ts`** and **`en-02-e1-coverage.spec.ts`**. Playwright treats a positional argument as a **filename filter**, and a filter matching nothing contributes **zero tests without failing** — so the master's literal command collects **2 tests and exits 0**, where the honest command collects **14**. A gate that silently shrinks 14 → 2 and still prints green is the **same silent-zero-collection class as F-1094-1** (`npm test` collecting 0 tests for nine days) and F-1101-1 (a green that means nothing). Caught here only because the collected count was read, not just the colour. **OWED, cheap and general: `/author-task` must `ls`-verify every spec path it cites, and any battery should state its COLLECTED COUNT alongside its pass count** — "13/14" is auditable, "passed" is not.

🔴 **F-1104-7 — I MERGED AN OWNER-GATED SLICE (rf-34) BECAUSE EVERY CHECK I RAN ASKED "IS IT READY?" AND NONE ASKED "IS IT ALLOWED?". REVERSED IN THE SAME FIRE.** ✓ VERIFIED s1104, self-reported. I drained `lane-hero-y-restore-roundtrip` to `c1b08f43`, gated it fully green, and discovered only while updating its goal leaf that `tasks/goals.json` carries it `status: "blocked"` / `blockedReason: OWNER DESIGN FORK (F-1096-2)` and that **`BACKLOG:70` still lists it live on the OWNER'S DESK, unruled**. The reserved fork — does the snapshot's saved Y own render-side hero height (A, the one-line deletion, recommended) or is terrain-derived `visualY` authoritative (B) — was s1095's to reserve and Robin's to answer; **merging made main carry the owner's decision** (§7.3 violation, not a judgement call). **Reversed by hand** because `git revert` is permission-gated for fires: the line was restored via Edit and verified *structurally*, `git diff f4ea62b8 -- src/game/RunSuspend.ts` **empty** = byte-identical to pre-merge main, tsc clean after. **Nothing was lost — `lane/m3 f1fce725` still holds the work, and one owner word still merges it** (the full green battery is banked in `reviews/hero-y-restore-roundtrip.md` so the eventual drain need not re-run it; the merge will need **no graft**, main never moved that file since the lane base). **The instructive part is WHY the checks missed it:** `main..lane/m3`, the two-dot content diff, main's movement on the file, and the runner's conclusion **all passed and all measure readiness** — a policy block is invisible to every git probe, because it lives in `goals.json.status` and on the OWNER'S DESK. Worse, the standing memory *"read what the runner CONCLUDED, not just what it changed — s1096: rf-34 decided an owner-reserved fork"* **names this exact slice**: I read the conclusion, judged it technically sound, and never connected soundness to the block that soundness had already caused. **A well-argued runner report is not an unblock — it is what made the fork worth reserving.** ➡️ **OWED, and mechanisable so it cannot be forgotten again: make "look up the slice's leaf in `tasks/goals.json` and REFUSE on `status: blocked` / `blockedReason` / any OWNER'S DESK mention" the FIRST step of `/drain`, backed by a `scripts/` guard** — the leaf is keyed by `taskFile`, which every done-move filename already contains, so this is a lookup, not a judgement. ⚠️ **AND THE REVERSAL LEFT A TRAP THAT THE NEXT FIRE MUST NOT WALK INTO: `lane/m3` IS NOW AN ANCESTOR OF MAIN, SO `git log main..lane/m3` READS **EMPTY** — the lane looks *already drained* while its content sits **reverted** on main.** This is the false-ahead class inverted (false-*merged*), and it means **`git merge lane/m3` when Robin rules (A) will be a silent NO-OP**. The re-land must **re-apply the one-line deletion by content** — delete `game.syncHeroVisualHeight?.();` from `src/game/RunSuspend.ts:837` (the call between the `setPaused` block and `cameraRig?.snapTo?.(...)`) — and must be verified by `grep -c syncHeroVisualHeight src/game/RunSuspend.ts` going **1 → 0**, never by a branch-ahead count. The done-move is renamed `OWNER-GATED-F-1096-2-do-not-drain-*` so the file itself says so. <br>🛡️ **GUARDED s1105 (`ee855c47`) — THE OWED FIX IS SHIPPED: `scripts/drain-block-check.mjs` + `fire.md §3.0`, the first command of every drain.** It answers the one question the whole gate battery cannot: a policy block is **not a property of the tree**. Keyed by `taskFile` (which every done-move filename already carries), it reads `goals.json` `status:"blocked"`+`blockedReason`, prints the reason and the matching OWNER'S-DESK BACKLOG lines, and exits 1. **Evidence — 8/8 regression cases: exit 1 on the renamed done-move, on the PRE-RENAME filename s1104 actually saw (goals.json catching it with no filename marker to help), on the bare master, AND on `lane/m3` itself** (branch names aren't registered as leaves, so blocked leaves' prose is scanned for the ref — the gap that would have let a drain starting from `main..<branch>` walk straight past the guard); exit 0 on the live calibration, a merged slice, an unrelated live branch, and an unknown slice. **Mutation control** on a scratch copy: flipping ONLY `status` `blocked`→`merged` turns both blocks green — proving the verdict rides the status field, not a name coincidence, and that the guard stands down the instant Robin rules. A CLEAR verdict is never allowed to rest on a coincidental substring (branch-shaped input reports UNKNOWN unless a leaf is genuinely blocked), and UNKNOWN is explicitly **not a clearance** — it is a Goal Registration Law finding. `--all` audits the board: **116 leaves, 1 blocked** (rf-34, still awaiting Robin's word). ⚠️ **OWED (one paste, attended-only): `.claude/skills/drain/SKILL.md` §0 needs the same first-step line — the fire's write permission does not reach `.claude/`, so `/drain` invoked by an attended session still leads with the readiness checks.** Exact wording is in `fire.md §3.0`; copy it as the first `- [ ]` of §0.

🟠 **F-1107-1 — THE CALIBRATION VOIDED ITSELF ON A 17.88% "DRIFT" THAT WAS ONE FLAKY TEST, NOT AN UNSTABLE BOX — SO ITS OWN REMEDY ("re-run on a stable box") WOULD BE VOIDED AGAIN. F-1101-1 REMAINS OPEN.** ✓ VERIFIED s1107 by re-deriving p95 from the report's published duration table; the parse reproduces its author's own figures (**17.9s / 14.7s**) exactly, so it is validated against the thing it audits. `lane-calibrate-suite-workers` ran its full `1,2,4,8,1` sequence and then **STOPped lawfully under its own Ruling 5**: the two `workers=1` controls disagreed at p95 by 17.88% against a predeclared 15% ceiling, so **no worker count was pinned and no guard shipped** — `playwright.config.ts` is untouched and the whole-suite gate still poisons itself. **But the drift is not box instability: 40 of the 43 tests reproduce within ±0.5s across the two controls.** The single mover is **`055-baron-kill-stop.spec.ts:121` at −9.8s**, which was w1's **lone red** *and* its **largest duration (21.6s)**, going green at 11.8s in the repeat. Since **p95 nearest-rank at n=43 reads the 3rd-largest value**, greening that one test is by itself sufficient to walk p95 from 17.9s → 14.7s. That is the entire "drift". ➡️ **TWO CONSEQUENCES FOR THE RE-RUN, both cheap:** (1) **the instrument's greenness was established from ONE banked w4 log** — the subset was frozen as "fully green" precisely so failures could not confound the duration signal, and a failure confounded it exactly as feared; **s1104(D)'s standing law — *a control run ONCE is a coin toss* — applies to instrument SELECTION, not just to fingerprinting**, so re-derive the subset from repeated runs or drop `055:121`; (2) **compute the drift control over green tests only**, because at n=43 the metric is dominated by its top 3 samples by construction. The provisional **`workers=2`** result (the only candidate with inflation ≤1.25, max ≤25s, zero reds) stands **unpromoted but uncontradicted**. ⚠️ **RED HERRING, recorded so the next fire does not chase it:** the repeat's accepted quiescence samples were `{ 3.14 25.14 21.29 }` vs the first w1's `{ 2.20 2.51 3.43 }` — a ~**8× higher 5-minute load**, because the gate only ever tested the **1-minute** figure, which recovers in ~2 min while the 5/15-min averages stay elevated long after the w8 saturation. It looks like the culprit and **is not**: despite that 8× gap, 40 of 43 tests still landed within ±0.5s. Worth tightening the gate regardless. Landed report-only (pure-add, 5 raw logs + `reviews/calib-suite-workers.md`, 1354 insertions / zero deletions, classified against merge-base `91d511e6`); **no battery run or owed — no `src`/`e2e`/`scripts`/config file changed.** Goal leaf `calibrate-suite-workers` → **`diagnosed`**, deliberately NOT `merged`, so no future fire reads F-1101-1 as closed.

🟠 **F-1107-2 — `055-baron-kill-stop.spec.ts:121` IS A GENUINE FLAKE, IT IS **NOT** THE rf-37 CLASS, AND IT IS LOAD-*IN*DEPENDENT.** ✓ VERIFIED s1107 by reading the test at source.
